 SHUTTLE_FEATURE_NAMES = list(distill_local.FEATURE_NAMES)
 # The 5 person-cue features (fusion_features.PERSON_FEATURE_NAMES).
 PERSON_FEATURE_NAMES = list(ff.PERSON_FEATURE_NAMES)
+SOURCE_MANIFEST_ENV = "RALLY_GOLDEN_SOURCE_MANIFEST"
+
+
+def is_full_fusion_feature_file(path: str) -> bool:
+    """True when an existing golden feature JSON already has shuttle+person cue dicts.
+
+    Corpus-growth runs can leave leaner windowing-only JSONs with the same filename pattern.
+    Those are useful for gts/windowing analysis, but they are not the replayable fusion
+    substrate that ``fusion_compare`` and ``ablation`` need.
+    """
+
+    try:
+        with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            data = json.load(f)
+    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError):
+        return False
+    candidates = data.get("candidates")
+    if not isinstance(candidates, list):
+        return False
+    if not candidates:
+        return True
+    return all(
+        isinstance(candidate, dict)
+        and "shuttle" in candidate
+        and "person" in candidate
+        for candidate in candidates
+    )
+
+
+def load_source_video_paths(source_manifest_path: Optional[str]) -> Dict[str, str]:
+    """Load ``video_id -> local_path`` from the collector golden-source manifest.
+
+    The collector records source/proxy ownership and full-file MD5s. Only entries marked
+    ``present_local`` with a concrete ``local_path`` can drive local GPU extraction.
+    """
+
+    if not source_manifest_path:
+        return {}
+    with open(source_manifest_path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
+        data = json.load(f)
+    entries = data.get("videos", data) if isinstance(data, dict) else data
+    if not isinstance(entries, list):
+        raise ValueError(f"source manifest must contain a videos list: {source_manifest_path}")
+
+    paths: Dict[str, str] = {}
+    for entry in entries:
+        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
+            continue
+        video_id = entry.get("video_id") or entry.get("name")
+        local_path = entry.get("local_path")
+        if not video_id or not local_path:
+            continue
+        if entry.get("source_status", "present_local") != "present_local":
+            continue
+        paths[str(video_id)] = str(local_path)
+    return paths
+
+
+def attach_source_video_paths(
+    specs: Sequence[Dict[str, Any]], source_manifest_path: Optional[str]
+) -> int:
+    """Attach explicit ``video_path`` fields to manifest specs from the source manifest.
+
+    Returns the number of specs updated. The collector source manifest is authoritative
+    for local source/proxy video location and overrides derived/stale manifest paths.
+    """
+
+    source_paths = load_source_video_paths(source_manifest_path)
+    updated = 0
+    for spec in specs:
+        name = str(spec.get("name", ""))
+        source_path = source_paths.get(name)
+        if not source_path:
+            continue
+        if spec.get("video_path") == source_path:
+            continue
+        spec["video_path"] = source_path
+        updated += 1
+    return updated
+
+
+def _filter_specs(
+    specs: Sequence[Dict[str, Any]], only: Sequence[str]
+) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
+    if not only:
+        return list(specs)
+    wanted = list(dict.fromkeys(only))
+    wanted_set = set(wanted)
+    filtered = [spec for spec in specs if str(spec.get("name")) in wanted_set]
+    found = {str(spec.get("name")) for spec in filtered}
+    missing = [name for name in wanted if name not in found]
+    if missing:
+        raise ValueError(f"--only requested unknown video(s): {', '.join(missing)}")
+    return filtered
 
 
 def _derive_video_path(spec: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:

Actually, looking at issue #4 more carefully: the default of `entry.get("source_status", "present_local")` when the key is missing—is this actually problematic? Let me re-read the docstring:

```python
def load_source_video_paths(source_manifest_path: Optional[str]) -&gt; Dict[str, str]:
    """Load ``video_id -&gt; local_path`` from the collector golden-source manifest.

    The collector records source/proxy ownership and full-file MD5s. Only entries marked
    ``present_local`` with a concrete ``local_path`` can drive local GPU extraction.
    """
```

The intent is "only entries marked present_local". But the code defaults missing status to present_local. This is a design bug IF missing status should mean "unknown" (not "present_local"). However, it could also be intentional: "assume local if no status recorded."

Given the ambiguity and that it's a design decision (not a logic error per se), I'll keep it but note it's questionable.

Actually, on reconsideration of #4: if the source manifest is authoritative and a missing key means "not specified in this manifest run," then defaulting to present_local is **reasonable** (assume if not marked otherwise, it's local). This is more of a design question than a bug. Let me check if there are any other concrete issues I might have missed.

Let me also reconsider the `all()` check at lines 60-65. The check is:
```python
return all(
    isinstance(candidate, dict)
    and "shuttle" in candidate
    and "person" in candidate
    for candidate in candidates
)
```

This checks:
1. candidate is a dict ✓
2. "shuttle" key exists ✓
3. "person" key exists ✓

But it does NOT check that the values are truthy or non-empty. So `{"shuttle": null, "person": null}` or `{"shuttle": {}, "person": {}}` would pass.

Is this a bug? Looking at the `extract_video()` function (lines 251-259), it creates candidates with:
```python
"shuttle": {k: float(v) for k, v in zip(SHUTTLE_FEATURE_NAMES, xs)},
"person": {k: float(v) for k, v in person.items()},
```

So shuttle and person should be non-empty dicts. If they're null, that's a data corruption/incomplete write, which the check doesn't catch.

Actually, maybe the right fix is to check that both keys exist AND their values are dicts (not null). Let me see if there's a way a valid full-fusion file could have null values. Looking at extract_video, it always creates non-null dicts. So null values = corruption or incomplete write.

The check should be:
```python
return all(
    isinstance(candidate, dict)
    and isinstance(candidate.get("shuttle"), dict)
    and isinstance(candidate.get("person"), dict)
    for candidate in candidates
)
```

OK, so issue #2 is valid: the key-presence check is insufficient.

Now let me reconsider if issue #4 is a bug or not. Looking at the code flow:

```python
for entry in entries:
    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
        continue
    video_id = entry.get("video_id") or entry.get("name")
    local_path = entry.get("local_path")
    if not video_id or not local_path:
        continue
    if entry.get("source_status", "present_local") != "present_local":
        continue
    paths[str(video_id)] = str(local_path)
```

The default of `"present_local"` when source_status is missing means: **include entries that have no explicit status**. This could be intentional (assume local unless proven otherwise) or a bug (should skip entries without explicit status).

Without more context about the source manifest schema, I can't definitively call this a bug. But it IS suspicious: why default to an active status rather than a neutral skip? I'll include it but note it's design-questionable.

Actually, re-reading the docstring: "Only entries marked ``present_local``". The word "marked" suggests explicit marking. So defaulting an unmarked entry to that status is **contradictory to the docstring**. I'll keep this as a finding.

<result>Now I have a complete picture. Let me analyze the key efficiency issues:

## Analysis:

1. **Line 319 in fusion_golden.py - `is_full_fusion_feature_file()` double-read**: The function reads+parses the entire feature JSON (line 51-52) just to check if it has "shuttle" and "person" keys in candidates. This is called once per video in the loop (line 319), and the feature JSON can be large for a full match. However, looking at the actual flow (lines 316-337), the file is ONLY read once for schema validation—it's not re-read elsewhere for actual feature extraction. The `extract_video()` function creates a new JSON from scratch. So while expensive for large JSONs, it's a one-read-per-video operation.

2. **Line 299-300 in fusion_golden.py - Double stat call in lambda**: When `smallest_first=True`, the code calls both `os.path.isfile()` AND `os.path.getsize()` on the trajectory file. This is O(n) where n is the number of specs, but it's in a sort lambda (happens once, not per-iteration). Each file gets two stat calls instead of one (isfile check + size check could be combined). This is measurable redundancy.

3. **Line 106 in fusion_golden.py - `load_source_video_paths()` in `attach_source_video_paths()`**: This reads and parses the entire source manifest JSON, then iterates once through specs. Called once per run (line 286 in fusion_golden.py, line 50 in fusion_audio.py). No loop overhead—acceptable pattern.

4. **Lines 128-129 in fusion_golden.py - `_filter_specs()` redundancy**: After filtering, it rebuilds `found` set (line 128) from `filtered` list by re-extracting names via `str(spec.get("name"))` again. Already computed these names in line 127's list comprehension. Minor but unnecessary recomputation.

5. **Lines 60, 66 in fusion_audio.py - Double isfile checks per spec**: In the `augment()` loop, line 60 checks if the feature JSON exists, and line 66 checks if the video exists. Both files will be opened/read immediately after (lines 75-76 for JSON), so the isfile checks are reasonable guards, not redundant.

6. **Line 365 in fusion_golden.py - Single pass through candidates**: Computing `n_pos = sum(c["label"] for c in data["candidates"])` is a single iteration—not redundant since candidates haven't been iterated before.

```</result>
=== its body:
def _is_fusion_schema(candidates: Sequence[Dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
    """True iff every candidate row carries the per-cue feature dicts the golden-feature loaders
    read (``shuttle`` + ``person``; ``audio`` stays optional — it is added later by
    ``fusion_audio.augment`` and older JSONs predate it).

    The velocity-windowing / distill corpus-growth path persists a LEANER
    ``*_golden_features.json`` row (``start``/``end`` + velocity stats, no cue dicts) for clips
    that never went through fusion feature extraction. The loaders that build cue-rich
    ``VideoCandidates`` from these files — ``ablation.load_videos``, ``fusion_compare.load_videos``
    and ``fusion_audio.load_videos_with_audio`` — SKIP those rather than crash on ``c["shuttle"]``,
    so a MIXED corpus (some fusion files, some windowing-only) loads the fusion subset cleanly.
    An empty candidate list is not a fusion file either.

    Single source of truth for the three loaders (consolidated 2026-06-18 after PRs #209/#210
    each landed an independent copy)."""
    return bool(candidates) and all(
        "shuttle" in c and "person" in c for c in candidates
    )

 ```
 The exporter writes `<out-dir>/<sport>/<video_id>.csv` (decimal-second `start,end` rows) and a `manifest.json` pairing each CSV with its video `local_path`/`video_url`, fps, resolution, and license. The indexer keys videos by **file MD5**, not by our `video_id`, so the downstream eval/training run must process the exact video file named in the manifest. Flags: `--sport` (restrict to one sport), `--include-staging` (default: curated only), `--include-noncommercial` (default: commercial-safe only).

      "body": "**Correctness + reuse: `is_full_fusion_feature_file` contradicts the documented single-source-of-truth and opens a silent corpus gap.**\n\nThe repo already has `backend/eval/experiment.py::_is_fusion_schema(candidates)` — explicitly documented as the *\"Single source of truth for the three loaders (consolidated 2026-06-18 after PRs #209/#210 each landed an independent copy)\"*, and its docstring states *\"An empty candidate list is not a fusion file either.\"* Its body is `return bool(candidates) and all(...)` → **empty candidates ⇒ False**.\n\nThis new function does the opposite: `if not candidates: return True`. That disagreement is not cosmetic — it produces a silent gap:\n- A video that extracts to **0 candidate windows** (reachable — the A7 log shows `mahadevpura_1` at just 1 window; a 0-window clip is a legit `extract_video` output, written as `candidates: []`) is classified full-fusion here → `run()`'s resume **skips re-extraction forever** (appends `out`, continues).\n- But every downstream loader (`ablation.load_videos`, `fusion_compare.load_videos`, `fusion_audio.load_videos_with_audio` → all via `experiment.load_golden_videos` → `_is_fusion_schema`) treats empty candidates as **non-fusion and skips the video**.\n- Net: the video is cached as \"done\" yet invisible to every analysis — a silent corpus hole.\n\n**Fix:** reuse the SSOT — call `experiment._is_fusion_schema(candidates)` and drop the `if not candidates: return True` special case. That also retires the third independent copy the #209/#210 consolidation was meant to prevent."
    },
    {
      "path": "backend/eval/fusion_audio.py",
      "line": 68,
      "side": "RIGHT",
      "body": "**Robustness: the new `FileNotFoundError` fires mid-loop, after partial in-place writes.** `augment()` writes each `<name>_golden_features.json` in place as it iterates; this guard raises on the first spec whose source video is missing — so specs 0..N-1 are already audio-augmented on disk while N..M are not, with no automatic resume (the caller must hand-build `--only` for the remainder). Same shape in `fusion_golden.extract_video` ([:179](backend/eval/fusion_golden.py#L179)). Failing loud is right; failing loud **after** partial mutation isn't. Pre-validate before the loop:\n```python\nmissing = [s for s in specs if not os.path.isfile(_derive_video_path(s))]\nif missing:\n    raise FileNotFoundError(f\"source video(s) missing: {[s.get('name') for s in missing]}\")\n```\nso the batch aborts before touching any output."
    },
    {
      "path": "backend/eval/fusion_golden.py",
      "line": 322,
      "side": "RIGHT",

And its `--help` (\"skip videos whose existing JSON already has full shuttle+person fusion schema\") is exactly what the default path already does. Either delete the flag + this branch, or give it real semantics (e.g. `--missing-fusion-only` = *only* touch videos lacking full fusion, skipping the windowing-only re-extract that the default now does)."

## [PR #458](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/458) — A7/A8/A9 quality-chain tooling · [review](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/458#pullrequestreview-4628578170)

Solid tooling (source-manifest attach, `--only`, thermal-abort — and I confirmed the plumbing is live end-to-end). **4 findings, verified:**

1. **🔴 Real bug (correctness + reuse):** the new `is_full_fusion_feature_file` returns `True` for empty candidates, contradicting the *documented single-source-of-truth* `experiment._is_fusion_schema` (returns `False`, consolidated after #209/#210 for exactly this reason). A 0-candidate video gets cached as "done" by `run()` yet excluded by every downstream loader → **silent corpus gap**. Fix: reuse the SSOT.
2. **Robustness:** the new `FileNotFoundError` in `augment`/`extract_video` fires *mid-loop* after partial in-place writes — pre-validate before the loop.
3. **Dead flag:** `--missing-fusion-only` is a functional no-op (only changes a log line).
4. **Test coverage:** widening the ablation litmus `== 6` → `in {6,15}` reopens the n=11 mixed-schema gap the test was written to guard.

## [PR #62](https://github.com/Khelsutra/sports-data-collector/pull/62) — n=15 source manifest · [review](https://github.com/Khelsutra/sports-data-collector/pull/62#pullrequestreview-4628581055)

Verified the good parts directly: **all 15 MD5s unique/valid, and the `video_id` set matches the eval corpus names exactly** (so #458's join won't drop anything). Nice cross-check — the `GX010141` MD5 is the exact hash my L4 run computed for that file this session. **Findings:**

1. **Provenance/coordinate-alignment risk:** GX010137/GX010141 record the full-res originals, but the eval used 1080p derivatives (different MD5, and the eval manifest says `frame_width=1920` while these 5.77GB/6.76GB originals are almost certainly wider). Pairing this `local_path` with the 1920-wide golden trajectory (which A7 does) risks a spatial coordinate mismatch. Record the eval-consumed file as authoritative.
2. **Provenance clarity** for the 5 `annotation_proxy` entries (no field says whether the eval used the proxy or an original).
3. **Body-vs-manifest drift:** the PR body says GX010137/141 are "missing / 13-of-15", but the shipped JSON marks all 15 `present_local` — update the description.
4. Minor test-robustness notes (unused role, `notes` KeyError, 9 new paths are trust-only).

The two PRs are coupled — #62's "record the original vs the eval-consumed derivative" decision directly drives whether #458's A7 features are computed on aligned coordinates, so I'd resolve #62's finding #1 before leaning on the A7 fusion numbers. Want me to dig into the actual GX010141 original resolution (via `ffprobe` on the F: file) to turn that coordinate-alignment risk from "plausible" into a definite yes/no?

### 796. assistant (2026-07-04T00:59:52.264Z)

**Confirmed — it's a real feature-corruption bug, precisely scoped.** The chain: `net_px` comes from the shuttle trajectory (1920-space, from the 1080p derivative), but line 250 divides it by `det_fw=3840` (the 4K original's width that `PersonDetector` reads), and line 255 normalizes shuttle points by `det_fw=3840` too. So for GX010141/GX010137 — the only two where the manifest points at a 4K original while the trajectory is 1080p — the net-relative and shuttle-relative **person-cue features are computed with a 2× coordinate mismatch**. The code assumes `det_fw == fw`, which holds for the other 13. Let me post this definitive evidence on #62, then build the #457 validation follow-up:

### 816. assistant (2026-07-04T01:03:39.739Z)

Both tasks are essentially done — here's the summary while the gate finishes.

## 1. The GX010141 resolution search — confirmed a real bug

`ffprobe` settled it: both Boxhill originals are **true 4K (3840×2160)** at 59.94fps, but the eval manifest records **`frame_width=1920`** (the trajectories came from `*_1080p.mp4` derivatives). fps and frame count match — pure spatial downscale — so it's a clean **2× spatial mismatch**.

And it's not benign: I traced it into `fusion_golden.extract_video` — `net_px` is in 1920-space (from the shuttle trajectory), but line 250 divides it by `det_fw=3840` (the 4K video width `PersonDetector` reads), and line 255 normalizes the shuttle points by 3840 too. The code assumes `det_fw == fw`, true for 13/15 videos but **not** GX010141/GX010137 — so their net- and shuttle-relative **person-cue features are computed 2× off**. That likely pollutes the A8 person-fusion verdict (ΔF1 +0.008, CI crosses zero) with 2 of 15 videos mis-scaled. I posted the [full evidence on #62](https://github.com/Khelsutra/sports-data-collector/pull/62#issuecomment-4880086107) — cleanest fix is recording the eval-consumed 1080p derivative (its MD5 is already in the notes) as `local_path` so `video_width == trajectory_width`.

## 2. The #457 config-validation follow-up

The reviewer was exactly right. Added pydantic `field_validator`s on `WasbIndexConfig`: `batch_size >= 1` when set, `prefetch_batches >= 0` (0 = off) — so a negative can't reach `--batch-size` as a bad DataLoader value or silently become depth-1 prefetch (the `max(1, int(depth))` clamp the reviewer flagged). Fails loud at config load / config.json instead of at GPU run time, with tests covering the reject + valid paths. Targeted tests + ruff + mypy are green; full suite is running now, and I'll push + open the PR + merge on green CI per our agreement.

I isolated this in a fresh worktree off `origin/master` because the shared checkout is currently sitting on a concurrent session's `codex/a7-a9-quality-chain` WIP — didn't touch it.

I'll confirm the PR and merge once the gate lands.

I verified all four of my original findings against commit `6214f63` — **all genuinely fixed**, not papered over, each with a targeted test:

- ✅ Partial-write abort → pre-validation moved before the loop in both `run()` and `augment()`
- ✅ `--missing-fusion-only` now has real semantics (upgrade-only), not a no-op
- ✅ Litmus re-coupled to the schema-predicate count

**But — and this is why re-review earns its keep — the partial-write fix broke 4 pre-existing tests, and #458's "Full test suite" CI is currently red.** The new pre-validation runs `_derive_video_path` + `isfile` on stub specs before the detector is stubbed; `test_fusion_golden_telemetry.py`'s `_write_manifest` creates `.csv`/`.rallies.csv` but not `.MP4`, so it raises `FileNotFoundError`. `ffprobe`: both Boxhill originals are **true 4K (3840×2160)** while the eval uses `frame_width=1920` → a 2× mismatch that `fusion_golden.py:250/255` doesn't handle (it assumes `det_fw == fw`), so person-cue features for GX010141/GX010137 are mis-scaled — likely polluting the A8 person-fusion verdict. ### 843. assistant (2026-07-04T01:13:25.385Z)

#459 merged (`a9f5848`) — that closes out the #457 review thread. Now, A1.

## Where A1 actually stands (this session de-risked ~80% of it)

A1's definition: *"Cloud Run/GPU, health check, edge auth ON, upload → process → jobs → compile → download verified."* The hard, unknown parts are now **proven**:

- **The GPU worker path works** — the litmus produced 48 real rally windows on the L4, fully-local, via the actual M6 dispatch shim (control-plane → L4 job → bucket done-marker).
- **The API surface exists** — `backend/main.py` already has `/api/health`, `/api/capabilities`, `/api/process` (202 + job_id), `/api/jobs/{id}`, `/api/jobs/{id}/cost`, `/api/compile`, FastAPI + `StaticFiles` for single-origin SPA, and the async-compile DB schema (#329) is in place.
- **CF Access token is verified-active** (DNS + Access edit scopes), and the khelsutra SPA already has the upload→poll→reel ingest screen.

What's missing is that I stood up and verified the **batch-job** path, then tore it all down — A1 needs the **persistent service** half stood up and the full page-driven CUJ verified end-to-end with auth on.

## The concrete remaining steps to flip A1 → DONE

**I can execute (≈half a day, ~$5–15 of the $300, mostly re-runs):**
1. Recreate the L4 job + serving bucket + Gemini secret (10 min from the runbook I wrote).
2. Deploy the **control-plane service** — the FastAPI app on Cloud Run (CPU, scale-to-zero → ~$0 idle), `DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving` so `/api/process` dispatches to the L4 job, serving the SPA single-origin.
3. **Flip edge auth ON (M9)** — create the CF Access app for `api.khelsutra.guru`, extract team-domain + AUD, set `security.edge_auth_enabled` + those values, lock Cloud Run ingress to the CF proxy, point the DNS CNAME. (I have the token.)
4. **Verify the full CUJ** with a real small clip: open page → auth → upload → process → poll jobs → compile → download reel. Write the runbook + rollback/disable steps (feeds A13).

**Genuinely owner-held (this is literally A1's gate: "Owner spend / CF config"):**
- **Spend go-ahead for a *persistent public* endpoint.** Standing cost is near-zero (scale-to-zero; GPU bills only per job), but a reachable public service is your call — you gated A1 on exactly this.
- **The invite allowlist** — who the CF Access policy lets in for the first wave.

## Two real product constraints A1 forces you to decide

1. **The 2 GB upload cap (D16).** Real GoPro matches are 4K and 5–11 GB (the litmus was 5.77 GB) — over the hard browser-upload cap. 97	    """Run ONE job already claimed (`status='running'`) by `claim_due_job`, recording its

99	
100	    Job `status` is the EXECUTION state (queued|waiting|running|done|failed): 'done' means
101	    the worker finished — the pipeline's own verdict (validation failure etc.) lives in
102	    result["status"]; 'failed' means the worker itself crashed/raised; 'waiting' means the
103	    job self-scheduled and will be re-claimed when `next_poll_at` is due.
104	    """
105	    import backend.main as _main
106	
107	    job_id = job["id"]
108	    # Dispatch by kind: 'compile' (#329, the async reel stitch) vs 'process' (NULL/default — the
109	    # original /api/process pipeline). Both record their terminal state the same way; only process

148	    job: Dict[str, Any], result: Dict[str, Any], config: Any, db: Any
149	) -> None:
150	    """M8 cost ledger (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M8, D8). DEFAULT-OFF: a no-op unless
151	    ``config.billing.enabled``. When on, compute the job's cost from ``result['usage']``
152	    (= {gpu_seconds, wall_seconds, egress_gb, gemini_usd, gpu_type}) × the versioned pricebook, and
153	    persist it.
154	
155	    ⚠ **Honest gap (M8 scope):** the pipeline does **not** emit ``result['usage']`` yet, so even with
156	    ``billing.enabled=True`` AND a real calibrated pricebook, every recorded cost is **0** until a
157	    FOLLOW-UP wires the worker to emit usage (gpu/wall/egress/gemini). M8 lands the ledger + pricebook
158	    + the recording seam; the usage-emission step is separate (the metering hooks on the real run).
159	    With the placeholder pricebook the cost is 0 regardless.
160	
161	    Best-effort — never fails a completed job because cost bookkeeping hiccuped. ``job`` is the

163	
164	    billing_cfg = getattr(config, "billing", None)
165	    if not billing_cfg or not getattr(billing_cfg, "enabled", False):
166	        return
167	    try:
168	        usage = (result or {}).get("usage") or {}

171	            usage=usage,
172	            pricebook_version=billing_cfg.pricebook_version,
173	            gpu_type=usage.get("gpu_type"),
174	            compute_backend=(result or {}).get("compute_backend"),
175	            owner_id=job.get("owner_id"),

212	def _drain_due_jobs(config: Any, db: Any) -> int:
213	    """One worker tick: claim and run every currently-runnable job (queued, or waiting whose
214	    `next_poll_at` is due), earliest-due first, until the table has nothing runnable.
215	
216	    Each parked job (`JobWaiting` → status 'waiting') drops out of this drain and is picked
217	    up by a FUTURE drain once due; we schedule that follow-up drain via `_schedule_drain` so
218	    a single-worker box keeps making progress with no central timer (the DB is the clock).
219	    Returns the number of jobs that reached a terminal/parked state this tick.
220	    """
221	    import backend.main as _main
222	
223	    ran = 0
224	    while True:
225	        job = db.claim_due_job()
226	        if job is None:
227	            break
228	        ran += 1
229	        _main._run_claimed_job(job, config, db)
230	    # If anything is parked-but-not-yet-due, arrange a wake-up so it resumes without a new
231	    # submit. On the single-box executor this is a delayed re-drain; multi-worker just relies
232	    # on the next submit/drain. Best-effort — never raises into the worker.
233	    _main._schedule_next_drain_if_waiting(config, db)
234	    return ran

240	    due-ness, so an early wake is harmless and a clamped wake for a far-future job is fine."""

243	    try:
244	        delay = db.seconds_until_next_due()

Now I have the complete picture. The compile wiring is **code-complete and correct** — the reliability gap is Cloud Run CPU-throttling background threads, and `fail_stale_jobs` marks an interrupted compile as *failed* on restart. Two-part "no failures" fix: (1) deploy config `--no-cpu-throttling --min-instances=1` so the drain thread completes, and (2) a principled hardening — **re-queue** stale *compile* jobs on restart (cheap + idempotent → safe to retry) rather than failing them, while still failing stale *process* jobs (expensive GPU, don't auto-retry). 49	## 3. Wire the dispatcher (control plane)
50	The control plane runs with the **cloud-serving** profile (`DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving` →
51	`compute_target=cloud_run`). `get_compute_backend(config)` then builds `CloudRunCompute` from env:
52	```bash
53	export DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving
54	export CLOUD_RUN_JOB=rally-worker  CLOUD_RUN_REGION=$REGION  GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=$PROJECT
55	# A `worker infer` (or the API job path) with a gs:// video now DISPATCHES to the Job instead of
56	# running in-process; the container runs the SAME worker INLINE (compute_target forced local_gpu).
57	python -m backend.worker infer --video-uri gs://<bucket>/videos/clip.mp4 --out-uri gs://<bucket> --segmenter wasb_hybrid
58	```
59	**The AI handoff needs its key IN THE JOB env** (`GEMINI_API_KEY` — mount a Secret Manager secret
60	on the job), or run fully-local with `--set indexing.skip_ai_handoff=true` (candidate windows
61	emitted as rallies — the eval-parity mode). With neither, the segmenter used to bail to a silent
62	0-rally "success" **after** the paid GPU pass; the cloud-serving startup checks now fail this at
63	boot (`AI_HANDOFF_KEY_MISSING`, rc 2) before the video is even pulled.
64

### 898. user (2026-07-04T01:25:37.349Z)

1	// Drives the in-UI file UPLOAD half of ingest (tracker T4): chunk a local file up to the engine's
2	// /api/upload/* endpoints, report byte progress, then hand the returned server PATH to the existing
3	// processing back-half (useIngestJob.submit). Alpha users have a FILE, not a gs:// URI — this is the
4	// keystone that lets them point the tool at it from the browser.
5	//
6	// Kept i18n-FREE (returns stable IngestErrorCodes via classifyError, not prose) so it unit-tests
7	// without a translation context — IngestPanel maps codes to localized strings via errorText. Abort
8	// is an AbortController: cancel/unmount/a superseding pick stops the in-flight upload and the
9	// orchestrator best-effort DELETEs the server-side partial.
10	import { useCallback, useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
11	import { uploadFile } from "../api/client";
12	import { classifyError, type IngestError } from "../lib/errors";
13	import { log, newCorrelationId } from "../lib/log";
14	
15	export type UploadPhase =
16	  | { status: "idle" }
17	  | { status: "uploading"; pct: number; filename: string }
18	  | { status: "error"; error: IngestError };
19	
20	export function useUpload(onComplete: (path: string, filename: string) => void) {
21	  const [phase, setPhase] = useState<UploadPhase>({ status: "idle" });
22	  const ctrlRef = useRef<AbortController | null>(null);
23	  // Keep the latest onComplete without making start depend on it (a parent re-render won't restart).
24	  const onCompleteRef = useRef(onComplete);
25	  onCompleteRef.current = onComplete;
26	
27	  const stop = useCallback(() => {
28	    ctrlRef.current?.abort();
29	    ctrlRef.current = null;
30	  }, []);
31	
32	  // Abort any in-flight upload on unmount.
33	  useEffect(() => stop, [stop]);
34	
35	  const start = useCallback(
36	    (file: File) => {
37	      // A 0-byte "video" is never valid — reject before touching the network (reuse invalidUri copy).
38	      if (!file || file.size === 0) {
39	        setPhase({ status: "error", error: { code: "invalidUri" } });
40	        return;
41	      }
42	
43	      stop(); // supersede any prior upload
44	      const ctrl = new AbortController();
45	      ctrlRef.current = ctrl;
46	      const cid = newCorrelationId();
47	      setPhase({ status: "uploading", pct: 0, filename: file.name });
48	      log("info", "upload.submit_start", { cid, filename: file.name, size: file.size });
49	
50	      void (async () => {
51	        try {
52	          const res = await uploadFile(file, {
53	            cid,
54	            signal: ctrl.signal,
55	            onProgress: ({ sentBytes, totalBytes }) => {
56	              if (ctrl.signal.aborted) return;
57	              const pct = totalBytes > 0 ? Math.min(100, Math.round((sentBytes / totalBytes) * 100)) : 0;
58	              setPhase({ status: "uploading", pct, filename: file.name });
59	            },
60	          });
61	          if (ctrl.signal.aborted) return;
62	          setPhase({ status: "idle" }); // hand off — the engine processing phase takes over
63	          onCompleteRef.current(res.path, file.name);
64	        } catch (err) {
65	          // User-cancelled (abort) is silent, not an error.
66	          if (ctrl.signal.aborted || (err instanceof DOMException && err.name === "AbortError")) return;
67	          setPhase({ status: "error", error: classifyError(err) });
68	          log("error", "upload.failed", { cid, filename: file.name, error: String(err) });
69	        }
70	      })();
71	    },
72	    [stop],
73	  );
74	
75	  const cancel = useCallback(() => {
76	    stop();
77	    setPhase({ status: "idle" });
78	    log("info", "upload.cancel", {});
79	  }, [stop]);
80	
81	  const reset = useCallback(() => {
82	    stop();
83	    setPhase({ status: "idle" });
84	  }, [stop]);
85	
86	  const busy = phase.status === "uploading";
87	  return { phase, start, cancel, reset, busy };
88	}
89
